````markdown
# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## What this repo is

Research code for AgentCo-Op (paper v1: arXiv 2605.20425). Branch `v4` (local) rebuilds the
project around one thesis — see the design contract
[docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-cross-examination-design.md](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-cross-examination-design.md)
(cited as §N), the implementation plan
[docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-v4-core.md](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-v4-core.md) and
the summary in [docs/method.md](docs/method.md):

> When the evidence needed to answer a scientific question is distributed across independently
> operated agents that cannot pool their resources — each holding data in its own,
> non-harmonized form — cross-examination lets them (1) recover much of what pooling the data
> would give, without pooling; (2) do better than a fixed federated pipeline run on the same
> agent-built inputs; (3) with no reference answer or optimization target at run time.

Training-free and objective-free: nothing is fine-tuned and nothing is optimized against a score
at run time; claims are kept by agreement between independent resources (§2.5).

Other branches and tags are history, not dependencies: v3 (optimizing collaboration without
reference answers) is reproducible from tag `v3.4.1` (also `v3-final`) — its code (workflow DAG,
handoffs, `optimize/`, judges, BixBench, the GeneAgent shim, `replay_check.py`) is gone from `v4`
and its numbers stay in [docs/results.md](docs/results.md), [docs/baselines.md](docs/baselines.md)
and [docs/case_study.md](docs/case_study.md); `main` = v1 as published (reproducibility record,
`94c3750`); `revision` = v2 evidence-certification compiler; `origin/feature/ecps-preference-search`
= v2 preference search (not merged). `gh-pages` still shows v1 numbers. Never delete remote branches.

Working memory for the long-running effort lives in `task_plan.md` (Phase 6 = v4), `findings.md`,
`progress.md` (read them first when resuming).

### Where the evidence stands (keep this honest; update from `findings.md`)

Main tables use ONE backbone for every arm (gpt-6-luna; Codex `-m gpt-6-luna`); other models
only in the appendix. Arms are defined in spec §4.

- v4 has no results yet. Build status and the first end-to-end smoke (cost, wall-clock, site
  calls) are in `progress.md`; nothing is reported before the pre-registered runs of §9.
- Why v4 exists (v3, tag `v3.4.1`): collaboration loss at agent interfaces is real, but an agent
  that sees all the data beats every composition (pooled shape: Codex alone 0.79; BixBench: no
  team beats the best single agent). v4 claims gains only where data cannot be pooled; beating
  a single site is a sanity check, not evidence (§1).
- Spend before v4 ≈ $104.7 actual; v4 hard cap $180 actual (§10).

## Commands

```bash
pytest -q                                                   # offline; no API key, no agent runs
python -m agentcoop.cases.planted data/bench/onek1k/onek1k.h5ad   # freeze the Case 3 base once ($0)
python -m agentcoop.eval.planted fsdev dev 800 808          # build a planted suite (eval side writes answers)
AGENTCOOP_BUDGET_USD=180 python experiments/run.py --suite fsdev --tasks fsdev-800 \
    --arms ours single union vote ma_genome --draws 0       # -> runs/v4/<suite>/<task>/<arm>__s<k>.json
python experiments/analyze.py --suite fsdev --tasks fsdev-800   # grade + tabulate -> runs/v4/<suite>_analysis.json
python experiments/headroom.py --help                       # $0 headroom gate (§6)
python -c "from agentcoop.cases.planted import TASKS; from tests.test_invariants import assert_planted_task_boundary as check; [check(d) for d in sorted((TASKS / 'fsdev').iterdir())]"
```

Refactoring rule: a behaviour-neutral change must not change any store key or stored result.
Check it by moving a finished task's result files aside and re-running `experiments/run.py` with
the same arms and draws: every agent call must come from `runs/store` (no new store entry, $0
actual) and the results must be identical. A behaviour change is a new method version, recorded
in `findings.md` with its reason; after tag `v4-freeze` it also needs a new suite (§9).

Environment: `AGENTCOOP_BUDGET_USD` (hard cap on *actual* spend across the ledger — always set it
for experiments; 180 for v4), `AGENTCOOP_MODEL` (backbone for every LLM role; default
`gpt-6-luna`), `AGENTCOOP_GRADER_MODEL` (pinned, default `gpt-6-luna`, never follows the
backbone), `AGENTCOOP_EXTERNAL_MODEL` (external agents' model; default `gpt-6-luna`, does *not*
follow the backbone), `AGENTCOOP_WS_ROOT` (site workspaces; default `~/agentcoop_ws`; must stay
outside the repo), `AGENTCOOP_BIXBENCH_ENV` (the read-only conda env site agents analyse in;
default `~/miniconda3/envs/bixbench`, built by `external_agents/bixbench_env/setup.sh`),
`AGENTCOOP_LLM_DB` (ledger/cache sqlite, default `runs/llm.sqlite`). The API key is read from
`OPENAI_API_KEY` or `.secrets/openai` — never print or commit it. A non-default backbone writes to
`runs/v4@<model>/`; new models need a row in `llm.PRICES`, and their capabilities are probed once
into `runs/model_profiles.json`.

Working rules (from the user): debug on 2–4 tasks before any larger run and scale in steps; give
every framework change a stated reason and evidence in `findings.md`; Codex baselines use the
user's locally logged-in CLI (`~/.codex`, never an API-key login); keep the design and the
conclusions model-agnostic (verify key findings on a second backbone: `gpt-4.1-mini`;
`gpt-4o-mini` fabricated reports as an external agent).

Data is not in git. Case 3 base: OneK1K (Yazar et al. 2022) from CZ CELLxGENE Discover,
`https://datasets.cellxgene.cziscience.com/1e44db10-b572-46cc-adae-dcc7acd44ca6.h5ad`, saved as
`data/bench/onek1k/onek1k.h5ad` (4.4 GB, schema 7.1.0: 1,248,980 cells × 35,528 genes, raw counts
in X, Ensembl var index + `feature_name`, `donor_id`, Cell Ontology `cell_type`); the frozen donor
subset is `data/bench/onek1k/base.h5ad` + `base.json` (donor pools, sha256). Suites live in
`data/tasks/<suite>/<task>/` (`public/`, `sites/`, `hidden/`). v3 data: `data/raw/kang_2018.h5ad`
comes from `api.figshare.com/v2/file/download/34464122` (the `figshare.com/ndownloader` URL is
behind a WAF challenge and returns 0 bytes); v4 does not use Kang 2018 (§7). Gene-set libraries
come from Enrichr's `geneSetLibrary?mode=text&libraryName=...` (Hallmark 2020, GO BP 2023,
Reactome 2022); marker libraries (CellMarker 2024, PanglaoDB) are in `data/markers/`. `runs/`
(ledger, store, results) is gitignored.

## Architecture

```
cases/planted.py ─▶ data/tasks/<suite>/<task>/public/ + sites/     eval/planted.py ─▶ hidden/ (answers)
      │ load_case → CaseSpec + site specs                                   │ grade (analyze.py only)
      ▼                                                                     ▼
coop/site.Site: workspace under WS_ROOT ─ sandboxed site agent (Codex | Finch) ─▶ view/ + claims.json
      │ site table (coop/view.py) ─▶ deterministic testers (coop/tester.py) ─▶ summary statistics only
      ▼
coop/protocol.run_protocol: propose → test → label_claim → resolve (Coordinator) → stop
      ▼
coop/arms.run_arms: ours, baselines, mechanism arms on one SiteHandle ─▶ ArmResult
      ▼
experiments/run.py ─▶ runs/v4/<suite>/<task>/<arm>__s<k>.json ─▶ experiments/analyze.py
```

- `llm.py` — one client: cost ledger, cache, hard budget cap, per-model capability adaptation.
  `proxy.py` — OpenAI-compatible local endpoint over it, so Finch's litellm calls share the
  backbone, cache, ledger and cap.
- `store.py` — `memo(key, compute, tag=)`: first-writer-wins memo of expensive agent calls with
  the nominal USD of each entry (charged again on every replay); `put_blob`/`get_blob` hold site
  views.
- `agents/` — `workspace.py` (`WS_ROOT`, APFS clones, `exec_env`, `sandbox_profile`,
  `sandboxed`), `codex.py` (Codex CLI on the local login, permission profile), `finch.py`,
  `react.py` (robustness runs only), `harness.py` (`run_agent` → `AgentRun`).
- `coop/` — `claims.py` (schema, validation, matching rule), `view.py` (site view files,
  validation, site table), `tester.py` (replication and scope tests, BH), `protocol.py` (shared
  label function, resolution, `run_protocol`), `site.py` (`Site`, prompts, file mailbox),
  `arms.py` (every arm of §4).
- `cases/planted.py` — Case 3 (§7): frozen OneK1K base, planted generator (3 proposing sites + a
  mimic site; knobs artefact and IDs+labels), `load_case`. It writes no answers.
- `eval/planted.py` — the only writer and reader of answers (`write_truth`, `build`,
  `load_truth`, `grade`).
- `experiments/` — `run.py` (runs arms, never grades), `analyze.py` (grades and tabulates),
  `headroom.py` ($0 headroom gate).

## Invariants — do not break

- **No path to the answers (§8).** `tests/test_invariants.py` fails if any module outside
  `agentcoop/eval/` imports `agentcoop.eval` or mentions `hidden/`, `truth.json` or
  `load_truth`. Suites are built from the eval side (`python -m agentcoop.eval.planted`); grading
  is always a separate step (`experiments/analyze.py`, `experiments/headroom.py`).
- **Site files are whitelisted.** Planted site files carry only `sample`, `condition`,
  `cell_label`, `assay` (opaque IDs), no var columns and no uns/obsm/obsp/layers; nothing in
  `public/` or `sites/` carries any part of the answer. Run `assert_planted_task_boundary` on
  every real suite before spending on it.
- **OS sandbox.** Every site-agent process runs under `sandbox-exec`
  (`agents/workspace.sandbox_profile`) or Codex's permission profile with the same deny list:
  the repo's `data/`, `runs/`, `.secrets/`, `CODEX_HOME`, and `WS_ROOT` except the agent's own
  workspace; network loopback only; Codex web search off. `tests/test_sandbox.py` proves it.
- **Workspaces live under `WS_ROOT`, never inside the repo.**
- **`inconclusive` and `untestable` are never support and never contradiction.**
- **`sample` semantics.** Same index ⇒ exact replay (store and LLM cache); a new index is an
  honest re-draw. Step-1 proposal keys contain no run id, so every arm compares identical step-1
  claims on identical views.
- **Nominal vs actual cost.** Method comparisons use nominal USD (stored with each store entry,
  charged on every replay); the cap uses actual USD. Every agent call is charged, failed ones
  included; a failure is recorded and never cached.
- **External agents run unmodified** (Codex CLI; Finch via `external_agents/run_finch.py`):
  change the prompt or the view contract, not the agent.
- **Freeze (§9).** Testers, δ/q, prompts, K, B, F, matching rules and scope tables are frozen on
  `fsdev` (tag `v4-freeze`) before `fstest` or any real case; no method change after the freeze
  without a new suite.

## Gotchas

- gpt-6-luna on Chat Completions: function tools require `reasoning_effort="none"` (then
  `temperature`/`seed` work); with reasoning, temperature must be default. Tool use *with*
  reasoning → `llm.responses()` (stateless: `store=False` + encrypted reasoning items).
- Callers pass *intent* (`reasoning_effort`, `temperature`); `llm._adapt_chat/_adapt_responses`
  map it per model. Never hardcode a model's quirks at a call site.
- Codex borrows only the login from the user's `CODEX_HOME` and is otherwise hermetic:
  `--ignore-user-config`, `-c project_doc_max_bytes=0`, throwaway `HOME`, `TMPDIR`/
  `NUMBA_CACHE_DIR`/`MPLCONFIGDIR` inside the workspace (else scanpy fails in the sandbox), stdin
  closed. `run_codex` strips `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `AGENTCOOP_*`, kills the process group on
  timeout and raises `CodexFailed` when Codex fails without output. Codex cannot be seeded:
  compare over ≥ 3 draws. It bills the local login ($0 actual; the API-equivalent cost is
  recorded as nominal); observed throughput ≤ 250 runs/day.
- The sandbox profile must allow the interpreters (conda base, the bixbench env, Finch's
  `.venv`) and the agent's own workspace: a profile that blocks Python startup makes every site
  look like a scientific failure. Seatbelt matches real paths (`/tmp` is `/private/tmp`).
- To force a site-agent call to re-execute after changing its harness or prompt code, bump `"v"`
  in the site's memo key (a behaviour change: record it). Deleting a result file only re-runs the
  arm on the stored calls.
- A task directory can be built only once (`build_suite` refuses), and the base refuses to be
  overwritten (suites record its sha256): results and store keys are keyed by task id, so build a
  new suite instead.
- `experiments/run.py` fixes the site → harness assignment per suite on first use
  (`runs/v4/<suite>/agents.json`; default Codex/Finch alternating over the site order) and never
  recomputes an existing result file.
- `nohup python ... > log` buffers stdout until exit; use `python -u` / `print(..., flush=True)`.
- Planting uses *core* genes (Hallmark ∩ family GO terms, ≥8 expressed); random Hallmark members
  are unrecognizable. Cholesterol/heme/DNA repair are rarely plantable in PBMC.
- Statistics unit is the task (planted suites), never cells or re-draws. Differences below 1e-9
  are ties.
- Caches are first-writer-wins (LLM: INSERT OR IGNORE + read-back; store: atomic link), so
  concurrent arms and runs converge on one value per key.
- Store entries are `{"key": ..., "value": ...}` JSON files under `runs/store/` (blobs under
  `runs/store/blobs/`); step-1 entries have `key["op"] == "propose"`.
- OneK1K is 1.2 M cells (4.4 GB): read it with h5py or backed mode; `prepare_base` streams it.
- `tests/__init__.py` keeps `tests` importable: the boundary check is imported from
  `tests.test_invariants`.

- 给我report的时候，始终要使用中文（只有给我report的时候需要，其他所有时候不受影响）
````

```markdown
# AgentCo-Op v4 — method (summary)

The design contract is the v4 spec,
[superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-cross-examination-design.md](superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-cross-examination-design.md)
(sections cited as §N); the implementation plan is
[superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-v4-core.md](superpowers/plans/2026-10-14-v4-core.md). This page
summarizes the protocol; where it and the spec differ, the spec wins. The v3 method (workflow
optimization without reference answers) is `docs/method.md` at tag `v3.4.1`.

## Setting and roles (§1, §2.1)

Evidence for a question sits at sites that cannot pool data, each in its own non-harmonized form.

- **Site agent**: an unmodified external agent (Codex CLI or FutureHouse Finch, on `gpt-6-luna`)
  bound to one site's private data in an OS-sandboxed workspace. It builds the site view,
  proposes claims and answers follow-ups.
- **Site tester**: deterministic code inside the site boundary. It reads only the site table
  computed from the view and returns summary statistics.
- **Coordinator**: an LLM plus fixed rules. It sees claims, test results, analysis specs and
  metadata cards, never sample- or cell-level data.

A site view (§2.2) is `genes.tsv` (native ID → symbol), `samples.tsv` (sample → donor, group),
`cells.tsv` (cell → population; scRNA) and `spec.json` (the site's analysis spec). A claim (§2.3)
is `{id, site, kind: de | composition, population, contrast: [target, reference], up_genes,
down_genes, effect, n_units, rank, rationale}` over the case vocabulary.

## Protocol (§2.5)

1. **Propose.** Each proposing site builds its view and proposes at most K = 10 claims in one
   call; tester-only sites build their view only.
2. **Test.** The proposer's own tester is a gate. The claim is then tested at every other site
   whose view holds its population and both groups (replication) and, per the case's fixed scope
   table, as a scope contrast (feeding only the scope rule). All non-proposer tests of a run form
   one BH family.
3. **Label** (one function shared by every arm; first match): `unsupported` (gate failed) →
   `disputed` (a non-proposer contradicts) → `corroborated` (≥ 1 non-proposer supports) →
   `site-specific` (the pooled non-proposer effect is absent) → `unresolved` (no other site could
   test it) → `inconclusive`. `inconclusive` and `untestable` never count as support or
   contradiction.
4. **Resolve.** Disputed claims and `untestable(genes|groups)` results go to the coordinator,
   which classifies the cause from the involved sites' specs and cards: analysis choice → both
   sites re-test once under the pre-registered default spec; handoff loss → one repair call to the
   site agent naming the defect, then a re-test; site artefact → that site's result becomes
   `inconclusive`; site biology → recorded. Follow-ups never show a site other sites' results; at
   most one per (claim, site); budget F = number of proposing sites. Step 3 is then re-applied.
5. **Stop** when nothing is disputed or view-caused untestable, or F is spent. Findings are the
   corroborated claims by rank (label tier, supporting sites, |Stouffer z|); every other claim is
   reported with its label and cause.

The protocol never compares alternatives by a score, never re-tests a claim twice, and uses a
re-test spec fixed in advance; thresholds are frozen on `fsdev` before any real case (§2.5, §9).

## Everything else

Testers and their parameters: §2.4. Arms (baselines, references, the $0 mechanism decomposition)
and the pre-registered comparisons: §3–§4. Cases: §5 (COVID-19, TB) and §7 (the controlled
planted suite). Headroom gate: §6. Invariants: §8, tested in `tests/test_invariants.py` and
`tests/test_sandbox.py`. Budget: §10.
```

- [ ] **Step 8: Rewrite `README.md` (v4 question, how it works, results pending)**

Replace the whole file with:

````markdown
<div align="center">
  <h1>AgentCo-Op</h1>
  <p><b>Cross-examination between independently developed scientific agents across resource silos</b></p>
  <p>
    <a href="https://ma-compbio-lab.github.io/AgentCo-Op">Project page (v1)</a> ·
    <a href="docs/method.md">Method</a> ·
    <a href="docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-14-cross-examination-design.md">Design (v4)</a> ·
    <a href="docs/results.md">v3 results</a>
  </p>
</div>

> **Branch `v4`.** A ground-up rebuild. The published v1 ("Retrieval-Based Synthesis of
> Interoperable Multi-Agent Workflows", arXiv 2605.20425) is preserved at `main` / `94c3750`;
> v3 (optimizing agent collaboration without reference answers) is reproducible from tag
> `v3.4.1`. The project page still describes v1.

## The question

The evidence for a scientific question may be spread over institutions that cannot pool their
data. Each holds it in its own, non-harmonized form and is served by its own, independently
developed agent. Can those agents cross-examine each other's claims and

1. recover much of what pooling the data would give, without pooling;
2. do better than a fixed federated pipeline (meta-analysis) run on the same agent-built inputs;
3. with no reference answer and no optimization target at run time?

A single site cannot see the others by construction, so beating a single site is only a sanity
check. The evidence is silo-cost recovery against a pooled oracle, the comparison with federated
meta-analysis, a $0 mechanism decomposition and a prior-only control.

## How it works

- **Sites behind a hard boundary.** Each site's data lives in a private workspace. Its agent
  (Codex CLI or FutureHouse Finch, unmodified, all on `gpt-6-luna`) runs under an OS sandbox
  (`sandbox-exec` or Codex's permission profile) that denies other sites, the repo's data and
  the network. Only summary statistics leave a site.
- **Agent-built site views.** In one call per site, the agent maps its native data (gene IDs,
  cell labels, condition codes) onto the case vocabulary as a machine-readable view. It also
  writes its analysis spec and proposes at most 10 claims in one shared schema.
- **Deterministic cross-testing.** Each claim is first tested by its proposer's tester. It is
  then tested at every other site that holds its population and contrast: donor-pseudobulk
  module scores, Welch t, a competitive gene-set null, BH over the run, and an equivalence band
  for "absent".
- **One shared labelling rule.** unsupported → disputed → corroborated → site-specific →
  unresolved → inconclusive. Inconclusive and untestable results never count as evidence.
- **Disagreements resolved by cause, not by score.** A coordinator reads the involved sites'
  specs and metadata cards and classifies each dispute, within a fixed follow-up budget, as one
  of: analysis choice (re-test once under a pre-registered spec), handoff loss (one repair call
  naming the defect), site artefact, or site biology.
- **Every baseline on the same interface and budget.** Single sites, union, majority vote,
  MA-genome (fixed federated meta-analysis), a Codex coordinator, group chat, debate, a
  prior-only control and a silo-violating pooled oracle.

## Benchmarks

- **Controlled multi-site suite (Case 3).** Real healthy PBMCs (a frozen OneK1K donor subset) are
  split into 3 proposing sites and a mimic site. Treatment programs are planted by binomial
  thinning, so the answer is exact and hidden from every arm. Two knobs add a site artefact
  (assay confounded with condition) and heterogeneous gene IDs, labels and condition codes.
  `fsdev` (8 tasks) is for debugging and freezing; `fstest` (32 tasks) is run once after the
  freeze.
- **Real data (planned).** Severe COVID-19 PBMC across four institutions, validated on held-out
  cohorts; active TB whole-blood signatures across hospital cohorts.

## Results

**v4 results are pending.** No v4 experiment has been run beyond debugging and smoke tests.
Numbers will appear here only after the pre-registered runs (spec §9). v3 results answer a
different question (optimizing workflows without reference answers). They are in
[docs/results.md](docs/results.md) and reproducible from tag `v3.4.1`.

## Quick start

```bash
pip install -e .[dev]
pytest -q                                              # offline, no API key
# data: see CLAUDE.md (OneK1K from CZ CELLxGENE Discover; Enrichr gene-set libraries)
python -m agentcoop.cases.planted data/bench/onek1k/onek1k.h5ad   # freeze the base once
python -m agentcoop.eval.planted fsdev dev 800 808    # build the dev suite
AGENTCOOP_BUDGET_USD=180 python experiments/run.py --suite fsdev --tasks fsdev-800 \
    --arms ours single union vote ma_genome --draws 0
python experiments/analyze.py --suite fsdev --tasks fsdev-800
```

This needs an OpenAI API key in `OPENAI_API_KEY` or `.secrets/openai` (for Finch, the
coordinator and prior-only) and a locally logged-in Codex CLI (for site agents; never an API-key
login). All model calls go through one cached, cost-ledgered client with a hard budget cap
(`AGENTCOOP_BUDGET_USD`).
````

- [ ] **Step 13: Run the 9 arms on `fsdev-800` (costs money; cap 180)**

```bash
python -c "import json; from agentcoop import llm; json.dump(llm.spent(), open('runs/smoke_fsdev800.before.json', 'w'))"
date +%s > runs/smoke_fsdev800.t0
AGENTCOOP_BUDGET_USD=180 nohup caffeinate -i /usr/bin/time -p python -u experiments/run.py \

    --draws 0 > runs/smoke_fsdev800.log 2>&1 &
echo $! > runs/smoke_fsdev800.pid
```
The run takes about 1–2 h. Sites run one after another: 4 step-1 calls, ≤ 3 ours follow-ups and 4 MA self-checks, each capped at 15 min, so the hard ceiling is ≈ 3 h. Poll it with a background monitor, not a blocking shell:
```bash
tail -n 5 runs/smoke_fsdev800.log; kill -0 "$(cat runs/smoke_fsdev800.pid)" 2>/dev/null && echo running || echo finished
```
Expected when finished:
- 11 result lines of the form `fsdev-800 s0 <key> calls=<n> nominal $<x> <s>s`, with keys `ours`, `single:A`, `single:B`, `single:C`, `union`, `vote`, `ma_genome`, `no_routing`, `no_resolution`, `ma_claims`, `prior_only`;
- then `spent: {...}`;
- then `real <s>`, `user <s>`, `sys <s>`.

Files: `runs/v4/fsdev/agents.json` = `{"A": "codex", "B": "finch", "C": "codex", "M": "codex"}` (the runner's default: codex/finch alternating over the proposing sites, codex for the tester-only mimic M; fixed for the whole suite from now on), plus 11 files `runs/v4/fsdev/fsdev-800/{ours,single-A,single-B,single-C,union,vote,ma_genome,no_routing,no_resolution,ma_claims,prior_only}__s0.json`. If the log shows `FAILED fsdev-800` with a traceback, or `BudgetExceeded`:
- do not run any other task;
- record the failure (Step 16);
- debug with superpowers:systematic-debugging, fixing the owning task's module with a test;
- re-run the same command. It resumes: existing result files are skipped and agent calls replay from `runs/store`.

- [ ] **Step 14: Grade the smoke**

Run: `python experiments/analyze.py --suite fsdev --tasks fsdev-800 | tee runs/smoke_fsdev800.analysis.txt`

Expected: one row per arm, with n = 1: `single:A`, `single:B`, `single:C`, `best_single`, `union`, `vote`, `ma_genome`, `prior_only`, `ours`, `no_routing`, `no_resolution`, `ma_claims`.
- Columns: precision, recall, F1, art.rej, scope, cause, claims, calls, nominal$.
- CIs print as `[-]`, because there is one task.
- The paired-Wilcoxon block shows n = 1 and `p=-`. Silo-cost recovery shows `n=0` and `-`, because the oracle was not run.
- `art.rej` is `-` everywhere, because `fsdev-800` has no artefact.
- It writes `runs/v4/fsdev_analysis.json`.

These numbers are descriptive only and are not compared.

Check `calls` against the arm definitions (Task 9):
- `single:*` = 1;
- `union` = `vote` = `no_routing` = 3;
- `no_resolution` = `ma_claims` = 4;
- `ma_genome` = 8 (4 step-1 replays and 4 self-checks);
- `ours` = 4 + its number of follow-ups;
- `prior_only` = 0.

Any other count is explained in Step 16. For example, a failed call is still counted.

- [ ] **Step 15: Measure cost, site calls, views and leaks**

`store.memo` persists each entry as `{"key": <memo key>, "value": <compute()'s dict + "nominal_usd">}`, so the script reads the operation, site and agent from `key` and the outcome from `value`. Step-1 entries are the ones with `key["op"] == "propose"` (Task 7's memo key); every other op is a follow-up (repair, re-test or MA self-check).

```bash
python - <<'EOF'
import json
from pathlib import Path

from agentcoop import llm
from agentcoop.agents.workspace import WS_ROOT
from agentcoop.cases.planted import CASE_VOCAB
from agentcoop.coop.view import load_view
from agentcoop.store import STORE

t0 = float(Path("runs/smoke_fsdev800.t0").read_text())
before, after = json.loads(Path("runs/smoke_fsdev800.before.json").read_text()), llm.spent()
print(f"actual ${after['actual_usd'] - before['actual_usd']:.4f}; ledger nominal (charged, replays "
      f"included) ${after['nominal_usd'] - before['nominal_usd']:.4f}; total actual now "
      f"${after['actual_usd']:.2f}")
for p in sorted(Path("runs/v4/fsdev/fsdev-800").glob("*__s0.json")):
    r = json.loads(p.read_text())
    print(f"{r['arm']:<14} claims={len(r['claims']):<3} calls={r['calls']:<3} "
          f"nominal=${r['nominal_usd']:.4f} run_arms={r['seconds']}s "
          f"followups={r['meta'].get('followups', '-')}")
entries = [json.loads(p.read_text()) for p in sorted(STORE.rglob("*.json"))
           if "blobs" not in p.parts and p.stat().st_mtime >= t0]
for d in entries:
    k, v = d["key"], d["value"]
    print(f"store op={k.get('op')} task={k.get('task')} site={k.get('site')} "
          f"agent={k.get('agent')} ok={v.get('ok')} seconds={v.get('seconds')} "
          f"nominal=${v.get('nominal_usd') or 0.0:.4f}")
step1 = sum(d["key"].get("op") == "propose" for d in entries)
print(f"site-agent executions: {len(entries)} ({step1} step-1, {len(entries) - step1} follow-up)")
for ws in sorted(WS_ROOT.glob("fsdev-800/*/s0/*")):
    view, errors = load_view(ws / "view", kind="scrna", vocab=CASE_VOCAB)
    try:
        raw = json.loads((ws / "claims.json").read_text())
        n = len(raw["claims"] if isinstance(raw, dict) else raw)
    except FileNotFoundError:
        n = "-"
    except (json.JSONDecodeError, KeyError, TypeError) as e:
        n = f"invalid ({e})"
    print(f"{ws.relative_to(WS_ROOT)}: view={'ok' if view else 'none'} errors={errors[:3]} "
          f"claims={n}")
EOF
git status --short
find . \( -path ./runs -o -path ./data -o -path ./.git \) -prune -o \( -name claims.json -o -name codebook.json \) -print
grep -nE "Traceback|FAILED|BudgetExceeded|CodexFailed|timed out" runs/smoke_fsdev800.log
tail -n 3 runs/smoke_fsdev800.log
```
What to check:
- **Spend.** Codex bills the login at $0 actual; Finch, the coordinator and prior-only are cents, so actual spend is a few dollars at most. Stop and report if actual exceeds $5 or nominal exceeds $20.
- **Store sharing (Review Focus 4).** Exactly 4 `store op=propose task=fsdev-800` lines, one per site (A, B, C, M), each with `ok=True`, and the summary line reads `(4 step-1, …)`. More than 4 means the step-1 proposals were not shared across the `ours`/`base`/`ma` families (the propose key must not contain the run id).
- **Executions.** Total site-agent executions ≤ 11 (4 step-1 + ≤ 3 follow-ups + 4 self-checks). The `agent=` and `seconds=` fields give the per-execution wall-clock and nominal cost for Codex and Finch separately (Step 16).
- **Workspaces.** 12 workspaces, `fsdev-800/{base,ma,ours}/s0/{A,B,C,M}`, all with `view=ok`. The view errors are the first measurement of the view-error rate (§6); `fsdev-800` has no ID/label knob, so expect none. `claims.json` exists in the `ours` workspaces of A, B and C (≤ 10 claims each) and is `-` for M.
- **Sandbox (Review Focus 5).** A site with `view=none` and no store entry is a failed call (failures are never cached). Read its log lines: an interpreter or import error points at the sandbox profile (Task 2), not at the science.
- **Nothing in the repo.** `git status --short` lists nothing beyond files that were already untracked before the run (e.g. `review.md`), and `find` prints nothing: no agent wrote into the repo.
- **Log.** `grep` prints nothing; every hit is a failure to record. `tail` shows the `real`/`user`/`sys` lines.
